accessibility_disclosure_accept = Agree and open settings
accessibility_disclosure_body = Monet is an Android TV launcher, not an accessibility tool for disability support. This optional service is used only for launcher behavior: it detects Home-key events, foreground launcher/system window names, and standby wake events so Monet can stay in the foreground after Home, after the stock TV launcher appears, and after the TV wakes.

Monet cannot read screen text or window contents. Service data is processed only on your device and is not stored, uploaded, sold, shared, used for ads, or used to control other apps.
accessibility_disclosure_cancel = Not now
accessibility_disclosure_title = Accessibility service
accessibility_guide_body = Some TV devices don't allow apps to open accessibility settings directly. Please follow these steps:

1. Open your TV Settings
2. Navigate to "Accessibility"
3. Scroll to the bottom of the list
4. Enable "Monet"

This allows the launcher to stay in the foreground when you press Home or Back.
accessibility_guide_dismiss = Got it

accessibility_service_description = Keeps Monet in the foreground on Android TV by handling Home-key and launcher window events. Monet does not read screen text or window contents.
accessibility_stalled_body = Monet's accessibility service is still listed as enabled, but it is no longer running. Your TV stopped it without starting it again, which is why the Home button now goes to the TV's own launcher.

Switching it off and on again fixes this:

1. Open the accessibility settings
2. Select Monet
3. Switch it off, then on again

adb_guide = How to set it up
adb_guide_body = First unlock the developer options:

1. Settings, then System, then About
2. Press Build number seven times
3. Go back and open Developer options

What comes next depends on what your TV offers there:

A plain on/off switch (Network debugging, ADB debugging or ADB over network): turn it on, come back here and press Connect. No computer needed.

Only Wireless debugging with a pairing code: Android does not let an app pair itself. Do it once from a computer on the same network with adb pair, then adb tcpip 5555, and press Connect here afterwards.

Your TV then asks whether to allow debugging. Choose Always allow, and that is it.

Tip: run Give Monet system rights right away. Those rights stay after a restart, even when the debugging port closes again.
adb_intro = Android comes with a debugging connection that you switch on yourself. With it, Monet can do a few things a normal app cannot: really close other apps, even on Android 14 and newer; turn on its own special permissions without hunting through menus; and set the default launcher even on TVs where the system dialog does nothing. It is free, the connection stays on this device and nothing is sent anywhere.
